L1496: ## 17.1.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Open-Label Extension, Study 011
L1497: **Table 76** shows TEAEs occurring at greater than 5% frequency during the OLE phase of Study 011, listed as MedDRA preferred terms. The most common AEs (&gt;20%) across 186 subjects exposed to nipocalimab through all phases of Study 011 were infections, headache, MG worsening, peripheral edema, lipid disorder, hemorrhage, diarrhea, rash, back pain, and muscle spasms. Overall, the AEs observed during the OLE period were consistent with those seen during the double-blind period.
L1498: Most events of PT hemorrhage were nonserious and localized hematomas/IV site bleeding, minor gastrointestinal bleeding (diverticulitis, hemorrhoidal), or minor hematuria. Subject (b)(6) was on concomitant anticoagulant treatment (apixaban) which likely contributed to the AEs of hemorrhage (Section 17.2.2).

L1525: ## 17.1.7. Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury, Open-Label Extension, Study 011
L1526: Figure 47 shows a screening assessment for potential cases of serious drug-induced liver injury in the OLE phase of Study 011. There was one potential Hy's Law case in the placebo-to-nipocalimab arm (Subject, [REDACTED](b)(6) with a fatal SAE of HLH, see Section 17.1.2). There was one SAE of acute cholecystitis (see Section 17.1.3).
L1527: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) had transient elevations in liver function tests (AST 228 U/L on OLE Week 16 followed by a normal reading of 38 U/L on Week 18 and again 168 U/L on OLE Week 36 followed by a normal reading of 21 U/L on Week 48). There were no associated symptoms or other liver function abnormalities. Treatment with nipocalimab was continued without interruptions.
L1528: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), a 68-year-old woman developed \ge 3\times upper limit of normal (ULN) elevation in AST and ALT in Week 24 of the double-blind phase that resolved by Week 8 of the OLE phase (AST peaked at 425 U/L, ALT peaked at 472 U/L). This was not accompanied by elevations in bilirubin or symptoms. Treatment was interrupted from OLE Day 1 and restarted on OLE Week 16, with no recurrence. Concomitant use of simvastatin was a confounding factor; however, a causal role for nipocalimab cannot be ruled out.
L1529: Figure 47. Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Study 011
L1530: Source: adlb.xpt; Software: R
Each data point represents a subject plotted by their maximum ALT or AST versus their maximum total bilirubin values in the postbaseline period.
A potential Hy's Law case was defined as having any postbaseline total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2X ULN after a postbaseline ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3X ULN. Those subjects who meet total bilirubin equal to or exceeding 2X ULN criteria within 30 days of the ALT or AST equal to or exceeding 3X ULN criteria are circled in red.
The within 30 days analysis window rule does not apply to cholestasis and Temple's corollary cases.
All subjects with at least one postbaseline ALT or AST, bilirubin and ULN are plotted.
In addition to central laboratory data, local laboratory data may be included in the analysis, if applicable.
Abbreviations: ALT, alanine aminotransferase; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; ULN, upper limit of normal

The LDL cholesterol levels remained within normal range during the active-treatment and long-term extension phases.

L1571: ## 17.3. 90-Day Safety Update
L1572: The Applicant submitted their 90-day safety update on November 26, 2024 (Janssen®Biotech 2024d), with an updated safety data cutoff date of August 23, 2024. The submission included updated safety data from the ongoing OLE period of Study 011 and the ongoing Phase 2/3 study in adolescents (Study 2001).
L1573: ## 17.3.1. 90-Day Safety Update, Study 011
L1574: As per the 90-day safety update cutoff date April 23, 2024, a total of 168 subjects have been exposed to the proposed labeling dose of nipocalimab for at least 6 months, and 140 subjects for at least 12 months (Section 7.5).
L1575: ## 17.3.1.1. Deaths, 90-Day Safety Update, Study 011
L1576: There was one additional death: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), a 73-year-old man, with a history of ischemic heart disease, aortic aneurysm, DVT, type 2 diabetes, and hypertension. On study day 183 he had an SAE of cholecystitis; see Sections 17.1.3 and 17.1.7. Then on Study Day 422 (1 day after the latest nipocalimab administration) he was found unconscious with respiratory and cardiac arrest, along with blood in the stools, and was not able to be resuscitated. The cause of death was determined to be heart failure as per the autopsy findings of “swollen and lesion-scarred heart with lung edema.” His concomitant medications had included prednisone and apixaban. It is unlikely that nipocalimab was related to this fatal event.
L1577: ## 17.3.1.2. Serious Adverse Events, 90-Day Safety Update, Study 011
L1578: In addition to the case of cardiac failure described in Section 17.3.1.1, there were 13 additional subjects with SAEs (n=1 except where stated otherwise) including, infection (upper respiratory tract infection, COVID-19, Epstein-Barr infection), abdominal pain, erysipelas, orchitis, diverticulitis, MG exacerbation (n=3), weight increased, lumbar spinal stenosis, and acute myocardial infarction. Additionally, Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) reported as having an SAE of MG worsening leading to discontinuation (Section 17.1.3), was updated to having an SAE of acute respiratory failure/cardiorespiratory arrest with successful resuscitation, as the event had occurred while hospitalized for respiratory syncytial virus infection and chronic obstructive pulmonary disease exacerbation.
L1579: ## **EBV Infection**
L1580: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), a 48-year-old woman on concomitant azathioprine and prednisone, developed pyrexia and myalgia on Study Day 630 (12 days after the last nipocalimab dose) in the OLE period. Nipocalimab treatment was interrupted. On Study Day 634, she presented to the emergency room due to pyrexia and myalgia and received antibiotics. The blood test result for EBV antiviral capsid antigen IgG and IgM and EBV anti-Epstein-Barr nuclear antigen IgG were positive. She was hospitalized for “fever probably due to EBV infection.” She was eventually discharged and nipocalimab treatment was restarted on Study Day 645. Whereas the IgG and IgM antibodies to viral capsid antigen can occur both in late primary infection and during EBV reactivation (Nystad and Myrmel 2007), the detection of the anti-Epstein-Barr nuclear antigen antibodies in this subject suggests EBV reactivation (Bernal and Whitehurst 2023).
L1581: ## 17.3.1.3. Adverse Events Leading to Treatment Discontinuation, 90-Day Safety Update, Study 011
L1582: There were three additional AEs that led to treatment discontinuation: granulomatous lymphadenitis (n=1), peripheral edema (n=1), and death from cardiac failure (n=1; Section 17.3.1.1).
L1583: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was a 25-year-old woman who developed peripheral edema and eyelid edema 11 days after the Study Day-269 dosing of nipocalimab. She discontinued study intervention due to peripheral edema, with last dose received on Study Day 367. She did not receive treatment, and the event was later reported as resolved. She had a medical history of nephrolithiasis and was on concomitant mycophenolate and methylprednisolone. The cause for the eyelid edema is unknown; angioedema or other allergic reaction cannot be ruled out. A causal role for nipocalimab cannot be ruled out.
L1584: Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) was a 61-year-old woman who had an SAE of pneumonia and MG worsening in the double-blind phase (see Section 7.6.1.3). She continued into the OLE, and on study day 272 she developed the nonserious event of granulomatous lymphadenitis of unknown cause of mild intensity; her last dose of nipocalimab was on study day 330 and she was discontinued from the study.
L1585: ## 17.3.1.4. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, 90-Day Safety Update, Study 011
L1586: The pattern and frequency of AEs observed during the OLE period with the 90-Day safety update data cutoff August 23, 2024, were consistent with those reported in the original BLA submission with the data cutoff March 31, 2024.
L1587: At the 90-day data cutoff August 23, 2024, the Applicant reported that the AEs of infections were the most common AEs observed in 71% of 176 subjects in the OLE period with frequent (&gt;5%) AEs included upper respiratory tract infection, respiratory tract infection (pneumonia, COVID-19, bronchitis, other related terms), urinary tract infection, influenza, herpes zoster. See Section 7.7.1 for discussion on infection and latent viral infection observed in nipocalimab-treated subjects.
L1588: There were five additional AEs of MG worsening during the safety update period. Most subjects did not have any action taken with nipocalimab.
L1589: There was one additional subject who experienced a nonserious hypersensitivity reaction; the reported terms for this subject include drug hypersensitivity/allergic reaction to lovenox/hypersensitivity/environmental allergies.
L1590: There were no new significant or clinically relevant changes reported in laboratory testing including chemistry, hematology, vital signs, or ECGs compared to those reported during the initial BLA submission. There were no new pregnancy exposure cases since the previous data cutoff date. See Section 8.4.
L1591: ## 17.3.2. 90-Day Safety Update, Study 2001
L1592: Since the March 31, 2024, cutoff date in the initial BLA submission, one additional subject enrolled in the active-treatment phase of Study 2001. Seven subjects have completed the 24-week active-treatment phase and from these, six subjects have continued into the LTE phase. Four subjects opted to switch from 15 mg/kg Q2W to the 30 mg/kg Q4W dosing regimen at variable timepoints in the LTE. By the time of this 90-day safety update, there were four subjects exposed to nipocalimab for at least 1 year. See Section 7.7.5 for further discussion on the exposures in Study 2001.
L1593: Subject (b)(6), a 16-year-old female, discontinued nipocalimab treatment in the LTE phase due to “lack of improvement.”
L1594: Subject (b)(6) was a 12-year-old male who developed two SAEs of MG worsening in the LTE phase. The subject had switched to 30 mg/kg Q4W at week 28 in the LTE phase. At week 36, the subject developed diplopia/strabismus and was switched back to 15 mg/kg Q2W dosing by Week 56. The subject then had two subsequent episodes of MG worsening (diplopia/strabismus) and was treated with IVIg and IV methylprednisolone. Nipocalimab 15 mg/kg Q2W dosing regimen was continued in the study.
L1595: Overall, the TEAE frequency and composition remained consistent with that seen in the adolescent and adult gMG population in the initial BLA submission. The most common (&gt;20%) TEAEs in the active and long-term treatment phases continued to be infections including nasopharyngitis, influenza, and COVID-19. Additional AEs reported in one subject each were
L1596: injection site reaction (“injection site swelling”), four infusion-related reactions in one subject (mild nonserious events of blood pressure increased) and rash.

L1601: ## 20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
L1602: Nipocalimab, a neonatal Fc receptor blocker, is a recombinant human IgG1\lambda monoclonal antibody. By binding to the neonatal Fc receptor, nipocalimab administration results in the reduction of circulating IgG levels. Nipocalimab has an aglycosylated Fc region, therefore it lacks effector functions. Nipocalimab has an approximate molecular weight of 142 kilodaltons (kDa).

The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.

2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	
The Division added a subsection, Recommended Vaccination (2.1), with recommendations regarding vaccination evaluations before initiation of IMAAVY. This inclusion of critical information at the beginning of the Dosage and Administration section is in alignment with other labeling in the class and with the recommendations included in the guidance for industry, Dosage and Administration Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products — Content and Format (January 2023).
In the Recommended Dosage (2.2) subsection, the Division added “via intravenous infusion” as the direction on administration of the dosage form (e.g., the route of administration) per 21 CFR 201.57(c)(3)(iv). The term “once” was added for clarity to the description of the initial dosage.
In the Preparation and Administration (2.3) subsection, the Division modified the instructions for patients weighing less than 40 kg to be administered a total volume of 100 mL rather than 250 mL to meet the tightened endotoxin acceptance criteria. The required verbatim statement for parenterals was added per 21 CFR 201.57(c)(3)(iv). The Division added the instruction to allow refrigerated diluted solution to warm to room temperature prior to administration for patient comfort.

In the Immunogenicity (12.6) subsection, the Division revised the introductory paragraph as stated in the guidance for industry, Immunogenicity Information in Human Prescription Therapeutic Protein and Select Drug Product Labeling — Content and Format (February 2022). Also, because of the low numbers of adolescent patients, the Division combined the immunogenicity data for adults and adolescent gMG patients.

13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY	
Under the Mutagenesis heading, the Division added the following statement: "As an antibody, nipocalimab-aahu is not expected to interact directly with DNA."
Under the Impairment of Fertility heading, the Division added the doses that were administered to monkeys and revised the safety margin to note that the highest dose tested was 20 times the recommended human dose of 15 mg/kg.

14 CLINICAL STUDIES	
To align with the revisions for the indication, the information [REDACTED] (b) (4) was removed from this section. The Division included baseline demographic information for patients in the efficacy analysis.

17 PATIENT COUNSELING INFORMATION	The Division added a heading with information regarding the pregnancy safety study.
Product Quality Sections (i.e., DOSAGE FORMS AND STRENGTHS, DESCRIPTION, HOW SUPPLIED/STORAGE AND HANDLING)	The Division revised the format of the strength presentation to align with the USP General Chapter <7> labeling recommendations for single-dose injectable drug products with containers that supply a volume of drug greater than 1 mL (i.e., the primary expression of strength is the strength per total volume [total drug content supplied in container] followed in close proximity by a parenthetical that includes the strength/mL). The suffix for the proper name, “-aahu”, was added in the appropriate locations. In the Description (11) section, the Division added that the drug is expressed in a genetically engineered Chinese hamster ovary cell line; revised the inactive ingredient list to the established names per USP/NF monographs titles; and ordered the inactive ingredients names alphabetically per USP General Chapters <1091> Labeling of Inactive Ingredients.

L1622: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1623: The following PMRs were required and agreed upon with the Applicant:
L1624: ## 4833-1
L1625: Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to IMAAVY (nipocalimab) during pregnancy and lactation to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of subjects will be specified in the protocol. The protocol must be agreed upon with the Agency.

L1629: Perform a lactation study (milk only or mother/infant pair study) in lactating women who have received therapeutic doses of IMAAVY using a validated assay to assess concentrations of nipocalimab in breast milk and to assess the effects on the breastfed infant, if available, based on study population.

L1740: Figure 1 is a scatter plot comparing observed individual serum nipocalimab concentration (in µg/mL) versus time since first dose (in weeks) for two groups: adolescents in Study 2001 (n=7, red dashed line) and adults in Study 011 (n=97, gray dashed line). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 1500 µg/mL, and the X-axis ranges from 0 to 24 weeks. Both groups show a rapid initial peak in concentration around Week 2, followed by a decline, and subsequent peaks around Weeks 8, 12, 16, and 20, before the concentration drops back to near zero by Week 24. The concentration profiles appear similar between the two groups.
L1741: Figure 2A: Line graph showing Median Percent Change in Anti-AChR Antibody Levels (H+L-IGR) over Time in Weeks (0 to 24) for Adolescents. The Y-axis ranges from -100 to 50. The placebo group (red line) shows a slight decrease, while the nipocalimab group (blue dashed line) shows a significant decrease, reaching approximately -75% at 2 weeks, and then fluctuating between -50% and -75% over the 24 weeks.
L1742: Figure 2B: Line graph showing Median Percent Change in Anti-AChR Antibody Levels (H+L-IGR) over Time since baseline (weeks) for Adults. The Y-axis ranges from -100 to 50. The placebo group (red line) shows a slight decrease, while the nipocalimab group (blue dashed line) shows a significant decrease, reaching approximately -75% at 2 weeks, and then fluctuating between -50% and -75% over the 24 weeks.
L1743: Figure 3: Two box-and-whisker plots comparing simulated steady-state concentrations of nipocalimab between Adolescent participants (N=7) and Adult participants (N=97). Plot A shows Predicted C_max at steady state (µg/mL), ranging from 100 to 700. Plot B shows Predicted AUC_tau at steady state (µg/mL*day), ranging from 400 to 1600. In both plots, the Adult participants show significantly higher median and interquartile range values compared to Adolescent participants.
L1744: Flowchart illustrating the design of Study 011:
Participants from Phase 2 MG Study undergo Screening (\le 4 weeks). This leads to the 24-week Double-blind Placebo-controlled Phase, which is divided into two arms:
Placebo Q2W IV
Nipocalimab 30 mg/kg IV LD at Week 0 and 15 mg/kg Q2W IV from Weeks 2 to 22
Subjects in the Nipocalimab arm proceed to the OLE Phase (Variable Duration†): Nipocalimab 15 mg/kg Q2W IV. Subjects in the Placebo arm proceed to Safety Follow-up at 8 weeks*.
L1745: Line graph showing LS Mean Change From Baseline in the MG-ADL Total Score by Randomized Treatment Group (Nipocalimab vs. Placebo) over time (Baseline to Week 24). The Y-axis is LS Mean in MG-ADL Total Score (ranging from 0 to -9). The X-axis is Time (Baseline, Week 1, Week 2, Week 3, Week 4, Week 6, Week 8, Week 12, Week 16, Week 20, Week 22, Week 23, Week 24). The Placebo group (red dashed line with triangles) shows a mean change generally between -2 and -3. The Nipocalimab group (blue solid line with triangles) shows a mean change generally between -4 and -5, indicating a greater decrease in MG-ADL total score over time. Error bars represent 95% CIs. The number of subjects with valid measurements is listed below the X-axis for each time point.
L1746: Figure 7 is a line graph showing the Least Squares (LS) Mean Change From Baseline in the Quantitative Myasthenia Gravis (QMG) Total Score over time (Weeks 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 22, 24) for two randomized treatment groups: Placebo (red dashed line) and Nipocalimab (blue solid line). The Y-axis represents the LS Mean in QMG Total Score, ranging from 0 to -5. The X-axis represents Time. Error bars indicate 95% Confidence Intervals (CIs). The Nipocalimab group consistently shows a greater decrease in the QMG Total Score compared to the Placebo group. L1747: Figure 8 is a schematic overview of Study 2001, detailing the Active Treatment Phase and the Long-Term Extension (LTE) Phase for two cohorts.
Active Treatment Phase:
Cohort 1: Adolescents (12 to <18 years). Screening (4 weeks) leads to the Active Treatment Phase. Dosing: Nipocalimab 30 mg/kg IV LD at Week 0 (Day 1), followed by 15 mg/kg Q2W IV from Weeks 2 to 22.
Cohort 2: Children (2 to <12 years). Screening (4 weeks) leads to the Active Treatment Phase. Dosing is weight-based:Weight 10 to <40 kg: Nipocalimab 45 mg/kg IV LD at Week 0 (Day 1), followed by 25 mg/kg Q2W from Weeks 2 to 22.
Weight \ge40 kg: Nipocalimab 30 mg/kg IV LD at Week 0 (Day 1), followed by 15 mg/kg Q2W from Weeks 2 to 22.

LTE Phase:
Subjects from both Cohort 1 and Cohort 2 enter the LTE Phase. Dosing is weight-based:
Cohort 1: Nipocalimab 15 mg/kg IV Q2W or Nipocalimab 30 mg/kg IV Q4W.
Cohort 2:Weight 10 to <40 kg: Nipocalimab 25 mg/kg IV Q2W or Nipocalimab 45 mg/kg IV Q4W.
Weight \ge40 kg: Nipocalimab 15 mg/kg IV Q2W or Nipocalimab 30 mg/kg IV Q4W.

Subjects in the LTE Phase proceed to a Safety Follow up at 8 weeks1.

L1785: Figure 46 displays two simulated profiles over time (days) for the Phase 3 dosing regimen in subjects with gMG. L1786: Figure 47 is a scatter plot titled "Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Study 011". The plot displays Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) (log transformed) on the Y-axis (ranging from 0.5 to 4.0) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) (log transformed) on the X-axis (ranging from 1 to 75). The data points represent subjects, categorized by treatment group: Placebo to Nipocalimab (blue squares) and Nipocalimab (red circles).
Key thresholds are marked by dashed lines:
A vertical dashed line is drawn at X=3 (Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN)).
A horizontal dashed line is drawn at Y=2 (Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN)).
Regions are labeled:
Cholestasis: The area where Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) is greater than or equal to 2.
Potential Hy's Law: The area where Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) is greater than or equal to 2 and Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) is greater than or equal to 3.
Temple's Corollary: The area where Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN) is less than 2 and Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) is greater than or equal to 3.
The plot shows that most subjects fall into the Cholestasis region (high bilirubin, low ALT/AST). A single red circle (Nipocalimab) is located in the Potential Hy's Law region (high bilirubin, high ALT/AST).
